Less is More Revisited Thanks: Work supported by: EPSRC EP/T006544/2, EP/K011715/1, EP/K034413/1, EP/L00058X/1, EP/N027833/2, EP/N028201/1, EP/T014709/2, EP/V000462/1, EP/X015955/1, NCSS/EPSRC VeTSS, and Horizon EU TaRDIS 101093006. † The full lyrics are found in Section 5 , Conclusion. Affiliation: University of Oxford, Oxford, UK
Nobuko Yoshida, Ping Hou

Abstract
Multiparty session types (MPST) [ 13 ] provide a type discipline where a programmer or architect specifies a whole view of communications as a global protocol , and each distributed program is locally type-checked against its end-point projection . After 10 years from the birth of MPST, Scalas and Yoshida [ 19 ] discovered that the proofs of type safety in the literature which use the end-point projection with mergeability are flawed. After this paper, researchers wrongly believed that the end-point projection (with mergeability) was unsound . We correct this misunderstanding, proposing a new general proof technique for type soundness of multiparty session π \pi -calculus, which uses an association relation between a global type and its end-point projection.



MnLargeSymbols’164
MnLargeSymbols’171

Association with Global Multiparty Session Types

1
Introduction

Today many computer science technologies are centred on

data science
and
machine learning
,
which fall within the field of technology
used to leverage data for creating and innovating products,
services, and infrastructural systems in society.
In 1996, C. B. Jones, together with J.R. Gurd,
predicted this era of
data
. In their article,

the Global-yet-Personal Information System
(GyP​
\int
​IS),
in

Computing Tomorrow: future research directions in computer science

edited by Wand and Milner
[
10
]
, they argued that
extracting meaning from data,
and understanding and building methods
that utilise data to inform predictions
will be the central concerns in future computing, proposing
the system called GyP​
\int
​IS. The thesis of GyP​
\int
​IS is
that data should be
globally
consistent,

open
for everybody, and yet
personalised

(structured with respect to an individual need).
They predicted that future systems would go beyond
the scope of the known programming paradigm, and argued for the importance of describing and
formally specifying
interactive behaviours
.

Multiparty session types
(MPST)
[
13
,
14
]
represent
a type discipline which
attempts to take a step to meet
the GyP​
\int
​IS challenge, by offering
a programming framework to guarantee
a global consistency among interactive agents or processes in the open system
[
11
,
20
]
.
It facilitates the description, specification and verification
of communications in concurrent and distributed systems
based on
multiparty protocols
.

A Global Type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
projection
(
\upharpoonright
)
Local Type for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

[formula]
Local Type for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

[formula]
Local Type for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}

[formula]
typing
(
\vdash
)
Process for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

[formula]
Process for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

[formula]
Process for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}

[formula]

Figure 1
:
Top-down methodology of multiparty session types

The design of multiparty protocols begins with a given
global
type
(
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
, top in Figure
1
),
and each participant’s implementation (process)
P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
(bottom)
relies on its
local
type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
(middle), obtained by
the end-point projection of the global type.
The global and local types reflect the global and local communication
behaviours, respectively.
Well-typed implementations (processes) that conform to a global type are
guaranteed to be
correct by construction
,
enjoying safety, deadlock-freedom, and liveness of interactions.
In this paper, we demonstrate rigour of this MPST
top-down

approach,
revisiting the work by Scalas and Yoshida
[
19
]
.

Misunderstanding on the top-down MPST.

The work
[
19
]
has proposed a general MPST framework
(called the
bottom-up
approach),
which does not require global types.
In the bottom-up approach, safety of processes
is enforced by directly checking safety of a set of
local types.
This approach offers more typability,
but is computationally more expensive.
For worse, in the asynchronous MPST
where processes communicating via infinite FIFO queues,
type-checking using the bottom-up approach is undecidable,
while in the top-down approach, it is decidable (as the projection is decidable).

The work
[
19
]
also pointed out that
proofs of some of the top-down typing systems, which use the projection with

mergeability
, are flawed.
The notion of mergeability was first introduced in

[
3
]
for modelling the end-point projection of
Web Service Choreography Description Language (WS-CDL)
[
9
]
.
Its aim is to broaden the typability of processes
by allowing more projectable (well-formed) global types than the
original limited projection
[
13
]
.
Mergeability is implemented in the Scribble protocol description language

[
12
,
22
,
23
]

and other related MPST tools.
Without using mergeability,
most global protocols are not projectable.
We formally define the projection and mergeability formally
in Definition
3
.

The statement in
[
19
]
has caused misunderstandings among researchers,
leading to claims such as “the top-down approach (with mergeability) is
unsound
”,
and subsequently, assertions that “global types are
problematic
”.

This paper proves that we can build a sound typing system using the
end-point projection with mergeability, and more importantly, a
utilisation of global types leads to a clear proof method for the type
soundness.

Outline.

§
2
recalls the multiparty session calculus from
[
19
]
;

§
3
defines global and local types, and
introduces
the
association relation

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
,
where global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and a set of local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
of session name
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
are related up to
subtyping
. We then prove the sound and complete correspondence
between global and local type semantics with
respect to
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}}
;

§
4
defines the typing system
using
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}}
and proves
type safety, deadlock-freedom, and liveness of typable processes; and

§
5
provides the conclusion.
The appendices include auxiliary material and proofs
of all the theorems.

2
Multiparty Session
\pi
-Calculus

This section presents the syntax of the synchronous multiparty session
\pi
-calculus,
and provides a formalisation of its operational semantics.

Syntax of Processes in Session
\pi
-Calculus.

The multiparty session
\pi
-calculus
models the behaviour of processes that interact
using multiparty channels. For simplicity of presentation,
our calculus is streamlined to focus on communication;
standard extensions, such as with expressions and “if…then…else” statements,
are routine and orthogonal to our formulation.

Definition 1
(Syntax of Multiparty Session
\pi
-Calculus)

The
multiparty session
\pi
-calculus
syntax is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\hskip 2mm}c@{\hskip 2mm}l@{\hskip 5mm}l}\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\hskip 5.69054pt&\Coloneqq\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(variable or channel for session ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}$ with role ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}$)}\\[1.13809pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}\hskip 5.69054pt&\Coloneqq\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(basic value, variable, or channel with role)}\\[1.13809pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}\hskip 5.69054pt&\Coloneqq\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(basic value or channel with role)}\\[1.13809pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\hskip 5.69054pt&\Coloneqq\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(inaction, restriction)}\\[1.13809pt]
\hskip 5.69054pt&\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(selection towards role ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$)}\\[1.13809pt]
\hskip 5.69054pt&\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}}}({x_{i}})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(branching from role ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$ with an index set $I\neq\emptyset$)}\\[1.13809pt]
\hskip 5.69054pt&\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\langle{\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}}}\rangle}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(process definition, process call)}\\[1.13809pt]
\hskip 5.69054pt&\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(parallel composition, error)}\\[1.13809pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}\hskip 5.69054pt&\Coloneqq\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}(\widetilde{x})={P}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\mbox{\footnotesize(declaration of process variable ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}$)}\end{array}

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},\ldots
denote
roles

belonging to a set
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s^{\prime}},\ldots
denote
sessions
;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y},\ldots
denote
variables
;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}},\ldots
denote
message labels
; and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y},\ldots
denote
process variables
.
Restriction, branching, and process definitions and declarations act as
binders, as expected;

\operatorname{fc}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\right)
is the set of
free channels with roles
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
, and
\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\right)
is
the set of
free variables
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
.
We adopt a form of Barendregt convention: bound sessions and process
variables are assumed pairwise distinct, and different from free ones.
We write
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi_{i\in I}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}}}
for the parallel composition of processes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}
.

A
session
is a sequence of interactions, typically including send and receive operations,
performed by a set of
roles
(
participants
) in a communication protocol.

The syntax of our calculus (
Definition

1
) is mostly
standard
[
19
]
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}
is a
basic value
(e.g. unit
()
, integers, strings).
A
channel with role
(a.k.a.
session endpoint
)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}
is
a multiparty communication
endpoint whose user plays role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
in the session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.

Inaction

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
represents a terminated process (and is often omitted).

Session restriction

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}

declares a new session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
with its scope restricted to the process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
.

Selection
(a.k.a.
internal choice
)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}

sends a message
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}
with payload

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}
to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
via endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
may be a variable or channel with role,
while
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}
may also be a basic value.

Branching
(a.k.a.
external choice
)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}}}({x_{i}})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}

expects to receive a message
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}

(for some
i\in I
) from role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
via endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
,
and then continues
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}
.

Process definition

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
and

process call

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\langle{\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}}}\rangle}
capture recursion: the
call invokes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}
by expanding it into
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
, and replacing its formal parameters with the actual ones.

Parallel composition

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
denotes two processes
capable of concurrent execution and potential communication.
Finally,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}
represents the
error
process.

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{rl}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-$\oplus\&$]}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}}}({x_{i}})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{k}}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}}}\;\;\to\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{k}}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{k}}}}}\right\}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\;\text{\footnotesize{}if\, $k\!\in\!I$}\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}$]}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\!\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{n}}}\right\rangle}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q})}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\;\to\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{1}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}}}}}\right\}}\cdots\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w_{n}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{n}}}}}\right\}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q})}}\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Ctx]}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\;\;\text{implies}\;\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}}\!\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\right]\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}}\!\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}}\right]\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Err]}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}}}({x_{i}})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}}}\;\;\to\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}\;\;\text{\footnotesize{}if\, $\forall i\!\in\!I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}\!\neq\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}$}\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-$\equiv$]}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}}\;\;\text{implies}\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 2
:
Operational semantics of multiparty session
\pi
-calculus.

\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}c@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R})}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\[5.69054pt]
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s^{\prime}}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s^{\prime}}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q})}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}}\;\;\text{\footnotesize{}if\, ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\!\not\in\!\operatorname{fc}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\right)$}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\[5.69054pt]
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}}\,\equiv\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}})}}\;\;\text{\footnotesize{}if\, ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\!\not\in\!\operatorname{fc}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right)$}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\[5.69054pt]
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q})}}\,\equiv\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}})}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\;\text{\footnotesize{}if\, $\operatorname{dpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right)\cap\operatorname{fpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\right)=\emptyset$}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\[5.69054pt]
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D^{\prime}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}})}}\;\equiv\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D^{\prime}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}})}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil\text{\footnotesize{}if\, $(\operatorname{dpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right)\cup\operatorname{fpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right))\cap\operatorname{dpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D^{\prime}}}\right)\,=\,(\operatorname{dpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D^{\prime}}}\right)\cup\operatorname{fpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D^{\prime}}}\right))\cap\operatorname{dpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right)\,=\,\emptyset$}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}

Figure 3
:
Standard structural congruence rules,
where
\operatorname{fpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right)
is the set of
free process variables
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}
,
and
\operatorname{dpv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\right)
is the set of
declared process
variables
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}
.

Operational Semantics of Session
\pi
-Calculus.

We provide the operational semantics of our multiparty
session
\pi
-calculus in
Definition

2
,
using a standard
structural congruence

\equiv

defined in
Figure

3
.

Definition 2
(Semantics of Multiparty Session
\pi
-Calculus)

A
reduction context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}
is defined as:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}\;\Coloneqq\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}D}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}[\,]
.
The
reduction

\to
is inductively defined in
Fig.

2
.
We denote
\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}
as the reflexive and transitive closure of
\to
.
We say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
has an error
iff there exists
some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}}\!\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}}\right]
.

Our operational semantics is standard
[
19
]
.
The reduction context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}
defines a process with a single placeholder
[\,]
, serving
as a substitute for a specific subterm
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
.
Rule
[R-
\oplus\&
]
describes a communication on session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
involving
receiver
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and sender
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, provided that
the transmitted message
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{k}}}
is
handled by the receiver (
k\in I
).
In case of a message label mismatch, rule
[R-Err]

is invoked to trigger an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}
or.
Rule
[R-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}
]
initiates the expansion of process definitions when called.
Additionally, rules
[R-Ctx]
and
[R-
\equiv
]
facilitate the reduction of processes
under reduction contexts and modulo structural congruence, respectively.

Example 1
(Syntax and Semantics of Session
\pi
-Calculus, from
[
1
]
)

Processes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
below communicate on a session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
uses the endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}
to
send an endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}
to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
uses the endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}
to receive an
endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
, then sends a message to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
via

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
.

\small\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{x}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{42}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}}}}\end{array}

By
Definition

2
, successful reductions yield:

\small\begin{array}[]{rl}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q})}}\\
=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{x}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{42}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}}}})}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-$\oplus\&$]}}\hskip-0.28453pt\to&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}{}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{42}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}})}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-$\oplus\&$]}}\hskip-0.28453pt\to&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\end{array}

Example 2
(OAuth Process, extended from
[
19
]
)

The following process interacts on session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
using channels with role
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}
, to play resp. roles
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
.
For brevity, we omit irrelevant message payloads.

\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}\right)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{where:}\left\{\scalebox{0.9}{\begin{minipage}[258.75pt]$\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}r@{\hskip 1mm}c@{\hskip 1mm}l}.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{cancel}}}}}}\\[2.84526pt]
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}]\&{\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{login}}\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{passwd}}\langle{\text{{{"{XYZ}"}}}}\rangle}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{cancel}}\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{quit}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fail}}\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fatal}}}}}}\end{array}\right\}}}\\[2.84526pt]
.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]\&{\!\big\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{passwd}}({y})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{auth}}\langle{\text{{{"{secret}"}}}}\rangle}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{quit}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fatal}}}\big\}}}\end{array}$\end{minipage}
}\right.

Here,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}\right)}}
is the parallel composition of processes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}
in the scope of session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
. In
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}
, “
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{cancel}}}}}}
” means: use
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}
to send
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{cancel}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
. Process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}
uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}
to receive
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{login}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{cancel}}
, or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fatal}}

from
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
; then, in the first case, it uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}
to send
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{passwd}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
; in the second case, it uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}
to send
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{quit}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
;
in the third case,
it uses
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}
to send
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fatal}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
.
By
Definition

2
, we have the reductions:

\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}\right)}}\;\to\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{quit}}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}\right)}}\;\to\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\right){\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\right)}}\;\equiv\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}

3
Multiparty Session Types

This section introduces multiparty session types. We provide
an extensive exploration of global and local types in
Section

3.1
,
including syntax, projection, and subtyping.
We establish a Labelled Transition System (LTS) semantics for both global types (
Section

3.2
)
and typing contexts (
Section

3.3
).
We explain the operational relationship
between these two semantics in
Section

3.4
. Furthermore,
we demonstrate that a typing context obtained via projection ensures safety, deadlock-freedom,
and liveness in
Section

3.5
.

3.1
Global and Local Types

The Multiparty Session Type (MPST) theory uses
global types
to
provide a comprehensive overview of communication between

roles
, such as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}},\ldots
, belonging to a set
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
.
In contrast, it employs
local types
, which are obtained via
projection
from a global type,
to describe how an
individual

role communicates with other roles from a local viewpoint.
The syntax of global and local types is presented in
Fig.

4
,
where constructs are mostly standard
[
19
]
.

\begin{array}[]{r@{\quad}c@{\quad}l@{\quad}l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{bool}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{real}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{unit}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\ldots&\text{\footnotesize Basic types}\\[1.42262pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}&\text{\footnotesize Basic type or Session type}\\[1.42262pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}&{\footnotesize\text{Transmission}}\\[1.42262pt]
&\mathbin{\;\big|\;}&{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\quad\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\quad\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}&\text{\footnotesize Recursion, Type variable, Termination}\\[4.30554pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}&\Coloneqq&{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}&\text{\footnotesize External choice}\\[1.42262pt]
&\mathbin{\;\big|\;}&{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}&\text{\footnotesize Internal choice}\\[1.42262pt]
&\mathbin{\;\big|\;}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\quad\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\quad\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}&\text{\footnotesize Recursion, Type variable, Termination}\end{array}

Figure 4
:
Syntax of types.

Basic types
are taken from a set
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathcal{B}}
, and describe types of values, ranging over integers, booleans, real numbers, units,
etc
.

Global types
range over
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G},{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}},\ldots
,
and describe the high-level behaviour for all roles.
The set of roles
in a global
type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is denoted by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}
.
We explain each syntactic construct of global types.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
: a
transmission
, denoting a
message from role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
,
with a label
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}
, a payload of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}
(either basic or local type),
and a continuation
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}
, where
i
is taken from an index
set
I
. We require that the index set be non-empty (
I\neq\emptyset
), labels

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}
be pair-wise distinct, and self receptions be excluded (i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
).

•

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{G}}
: a
recursive
global type, where
contractive requirements apply
[
17
, §21.8]
, i.e. each recursion variable
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}
is bound within a
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{\ldots}}

and is guarded.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
: a
terminated
global type (omitted where unambiguous).

Local types

(or
session types
) range over
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U},\ldots
, and describe
the behaviour of a single role. We elucidate each syntactic construct of local types.

•

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
:
an
internal choice
(
selection
), indicating
that the
current
role is expected to
send
to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

•

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
:
an
external choice
(
branching
), indicating
that the
current
role is expected to
receive
from role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}
: a
recursive
local type, following a pattern analogous to
recursive global types.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
: a
termination
(omitted where
unambiguous)

Similar to global types, local types also need pairwise distinct, non-empty labels.

Projection
In the top-down approach of MPST, local types are obtained
by projecting a global type onto roles.
Projection
is a
partial
function
that provides the local type associated with a participating role in a global type.
It takes a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and a role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, returning the corresponding local type.
Our definition of projection, as given in
Definition

3
below, is standard
[
19
]
.

Definition 3
(Global Type Projection)

The
projection of a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
onto a role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, written
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}
, is:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\left({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\right)}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}=\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\hskip 5mm}l@{}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}})\right\}_{i\in I}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$}\\[5.69054pt]
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}})\right\}_{i\in I}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}$
}\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$ \,and \,${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}$
}\end{array}\right.\\[22.76219pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}})}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}\;=\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0.5mm}l@{\hskip 3mm}l@{}}1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}})}}\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt&\text{\footnotesize if\,
${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}$ \,or\,
$\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}\right)\neq\emptyset$
}\\
1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt&\text{\footnotesize otherwise}\end{array}\right.\quad\qquad\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\hskip 1mm}c@{\hskip 1mm}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\end{array}\end{array}

where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{}}
is the
merge operator for session types
(
full merging
), defined as:

\begin{array}[]{c}\textstyle{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}\,{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\;=\;{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}})\right\}_{i\in I}}\\[2.84526pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}})}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\\[2.84526pt]
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}\,{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{\!j\in J}}\;=\;{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}_{k}}\right\}_{\!k\in I\cup J}}\\[2.84526pt]
\text{where}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}_{k}}\;=\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0.5mm}l@{\hskip 3mm}l@{}}1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt&\text{\footnotesize if\,
${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}I\cap J}$
}\\
1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt&\text{\footnotesize if\,
${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}I\setminus J}$
}\\
1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt&\text{\footnotesize if\,
${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}J\setminus I}$
}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

It is noteworthy that
there are global types that cannot be projected
onto all of their participants. This occurs when certain global types may describe
protocols that are inherently meaningless, leading to undefined merging operations, as illustrated
in
Example

4
following.

If a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
starts with a transmission from role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, projecting it onto
role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
) results in an internal (resp. external) choice, provided that the continuation
of each branching of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is also projectable. When projecting
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
onto other participants
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
(
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
), a merge operator, as defined in
Definition

3
and exemplified in
Example

4
below,
is used to ensure that the projections of all continuations are “compatible”.

If a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is a termination or type variable, projecting it onto any role results in a termination or type variable, respectively.
Finally, the projection of a recursive global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
preserves its recursive construct when projected onto a role in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
, or if there are no free type variables in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
;
otherwise, the projection yields termination.

Subtyping
We introduce a
subtyping
relation
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
on local types, as defined in
Definition

4
.
This subtyping relation is standard
[
19
]
, and will be used later when defining local type semantics
and establishing the relationship between global and local type semantics.

Definition 4
(Subtyping)

Given a standard subtyping
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}
for basic types
(e.g. including
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{real}}}
),
the
session subtyping relation
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
is coinductively defined:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}\qquad{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}^{\prime}_{i}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I\cup{}J}}\lx@proof@logical@and\forall i\in I{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}^{\prime}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{T}^{\prime}_{i}}}\par\\[8.61108pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\qquad{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I\cup J}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}^{\prime}_{i}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\lx@proof@logical@and\forall i\in I{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}^{\prime}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\\[8.61108pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}{}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}]\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\qquad\par{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}{}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}]\end{array}

Rule
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}
]
extends
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
to basic types.
The remaining rules establish that a subtype describes a more permissive session protocol w.r.t. its supertype.
Rule
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}
]
enables the subtype of an internal choice to
encompass a broader range of message labels, enabling the sending of more generic payloads.
Conversely, rule
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}
]
dictates that the subtype of an external choice supports
a narrower set of input message labels, and less generic payloads.
Rule
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
]
specifies that the type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
is its own subtype.
Finally, rules
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
L]
and
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
R]
assert the relationship between recursive types,
defined up to their unfolding.

Example 3
(Types of OAuth 2.0, from
[
19
]
)

Consider a protocol from OAuth 2.0
[
16
]
:
the
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
ervice sends to the
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
lient
either
a request
to
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{login}}
,

or

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{cancel}}
; in the first case,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
continues by sending
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{passwd}}
(carrying a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}
ing) to the
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
uthorisation server, who in turn sends
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{auth}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
(with a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}
ean, telling whether the client is authorised), and the session
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
s; in the second case,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
sends
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{quit}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
, and the session
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
s. This protocol can be represented by
the global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
:

\small{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}{:}\left\{{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{login}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{passwd}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{auth}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}}}}}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{cancel}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{quit}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}}}}}\end{array}}\right\}}

Following the MPST top-down methodology,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
is projected onto three local types (one for each role

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
):

\small\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{login}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{login}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}\end{array}

Here,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}
represents the
interface of
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
: it must send (
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}
) to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{login}}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}
; in the first case,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
must then receive (
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}
) message
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}
from
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
, and the session ends; otherwise, in the second case, the session just ends. Types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}
follow the same intuition.

Example 4
(Merge and Projection, originated from
[
15
]
)

Two external choice (branching) types from the same role with disjoint labels can be
merged into a type
carrying both labels,
e.g.

\small{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{farewell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{farewell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}\end{array}\right\}}

However, this does not apply to internal choices (selections), e.g.

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{farewell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}
is undefined.

Two external choices from the same role with same labels but different payloads cannot
be merged, e.g.

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}
is undefined. This also applies to internal choices.

Two external choices from different roles cannot be merged. Same
for internal choices, e.g.

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{B}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}

is undefined.

Additionally, two local types with same prefixes
but unmergable continuations
cannot be merged, e.g.

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{B}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{farewell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}
is undefined as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{B}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{farewell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}
is not mergeable.

Consider a global type:

\small{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{B}}}}}{:}\left\{{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{greet}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{farewell}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{farewell}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{greet}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}}}}\end{array}}\right\}}

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
cannot be projected onto role
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}

since:

\small\begin{array}[]{lll}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}}}}}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{farewell}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}}}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{greet}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}}}}}\\
&=&{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{farewell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{greet({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}})}}\text{\;\;({undefined})}\end{array}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}}}}}}
is undefined because in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
, depending on
whether
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{B}}}}
transmit
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{greet}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{farewell}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}
is expected to send either

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{farewell}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{greet}}
to
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}
.
However, since
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}
is not involved in the interactions between
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{A}}}}
and

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{B}}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}
is not aware of which message to send;
that is
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
does not provide a valid specification for
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{C}}}}
.

3.2
Semantics of Global Types

We now present a Labelled Transition System (LTS) semantics for global types.
To begin,
we introduce the transition labels in
Definition

5
,
which are also used in the LTS semantics of typing contexts (discussed later in
Section

3.3
).

Definition 5
(Transition Labels)

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}
be a transition label of the form:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}&\text{(in session ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}$, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}$ receives ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}$ from ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$)}\\[1.13809pt]
