&\mathbin{\;\big|\;}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}&\text{(in session ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}$, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}$ sends ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}$ to ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$)}\\[1.13809pt]
&\mathbin{\;\big|\;}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}&\text{(in session ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}$, message ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}$ is transmitted from ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}$ to ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}$)}\end{array}

The subject(s) of a transition label, written

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}
,  are defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}})}&=&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}})}\;=\;\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\right\}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}})}&=&\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\right\}}}\end{array}

The label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}
denotes a
synchronising communication between
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
via a message label

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}
; the subject of such a label includes
both
roles.
The labels

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}

describe sending and receiving actions separately.
Since we define a synchronous semantics for global types, these labels are
used for the typing context semantics in
Section

3.3
.

We proceed to give a Labelled Transition System (LTS) semantics to a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
in
Definition

6
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}{}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}/{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}]\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{j}}j\in I\\[8.61108pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\lx@proof@logical@and\forall i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}_{i}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}\cap\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\right\}}}=\emptyset\end{array}

Figure 5
:
Global type reduction rules.

Definition 6
(Global Type Reductions)

The global type transition
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}
is inductively
defined by the rules in
Fig.

5
.
We use

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}

if there
exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
;
we write

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}

if there
exists
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\not{\!\!\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}}
for its negation (i.e. there is no
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}

such that
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
).
Finally,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{}}{}^{\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}*}}
denotes the transitive and reflexive closure of

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}
.

Figure

5
depicts the standard global type reduction rules
[
21
]
.
Rule
[GR-
\oplus\&
]
models the communication between two roles.
Rule
[GR-
\mu
]
handles recursion.
Finally, rule
[GR-Ctx]
allows a reduction of a global type that is
causally independent from its prefix, provided that all of the continuations can
make the reduction of
that label. This causal independence is indicated by the subjects of the label
being disjoint from the prefix of the global type. For example, consider the global type

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{1}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}}}}}}
.
Given that the subjects of the label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}

are disjoint from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}})}\cap{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}=\emptyset
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
, rule
[GR-Ctx]
allows
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
to transition via
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}
:
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{u}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{1}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}}}
, which is unrelated to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
.

3.3
Semantics of Typing Context

After introducing the semantics of global types,
we now present an LTS semantics for
typing contexts
, which are
collections of local types.
The formal definition of a typing context is provided in
Definition

7
,
followed by its reduction rules in
Definition

8
.

Definition 7
(Typing Contexts)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}
denotes a partial mapping
from process variables to
n
-tuples of types,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
denotes a partial mapping
from channels to types. Their syntax is
defined as:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}\;\;\Coloneqq\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{n}}}}\quad\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\,\Coloneqq\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}

The
context composition

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{1}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{2}}

is defined iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{1}}}\right)}\cap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{2}}}\right)}=\emptyset
. We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}

iff

\exists{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}

(i.e. session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
occurs in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
). We write

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\!\not\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}

iff

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\!\not\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}

(i.e. session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
does not occur in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
). We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\right\}}}
iff
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
there is

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}
(i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
only contains session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
).
We write

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

iff

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}\!=\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)}

and

\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}{:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
.
We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\right\}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\right)}\!\subseteq\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
,
and
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}
(i.e. restriction of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
to session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
).

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}}}k\in I\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}}}k\in I\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{1}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{2}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{2}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}}})}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\\[5.69054pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\end{array}

Figure 6
:
Typing context reduction rules.

Definition 8
(Typing Context Reduction)

The
typing context transition
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}
is inductively defined by the rules in
Fig.

6
. We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}
if there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
. We define two
reductions

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, as follows:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
holds iff

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}

for any
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
(recall that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathcal{R}}
is the set of all roles):
this means that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
can progress via message transmission on session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
involving any roles
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
.
We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\!\!\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
for its negation
(i.e. there is no
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
), and we denote
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to^{\!*}}_{\!\!\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}
as the reflexive and transitive closure of
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
;

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
holds iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
:
this means that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
can progress via message transmission on any session.
We write
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\!\!\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}
for its negation, and we denote
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{}^{\!\!\!*}}}
as the reflexive and transitive closure of
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}
.

Figure

6
contains standard rules for
typing context reductions
[
19
]
.
Rules
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}
]
and
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}
]

specify that a typing context entry can execute an output and input transition, respectively.
Rule
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\&}
]

ensures synchronised matching input/output transitions, with payload compatibility through subtyping;
consequently, the context progresses via a message transmission label

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}
.
Rule
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
\mu
]
pertains to recursion, while

[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}
]
and
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}
]
address
reductions in a larger context.

3.4
Relating Semantics between Global Types and Typing Contexts

Following the introduction of
LTS semantics for global types (
Definition

6
)
and typing contexts (
Definition

8
),
we establish a relationship between these two semantics
using the projection operator
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{}}}}}
(
Definition

3
)
and the subtyping relation
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
(
Definition

4
).

Definition 9
(Association of Global Types and Typing Contexts)

A typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is associated with a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
for a multiparty session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
written
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
,
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}

can be split into two disjoint (possibly empty) sub-contexts

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
where:

1.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
contains projections of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\,\middle|\,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}}\right\}}}
, and

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}
;

2.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
contains only end endpoints:
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}}\right)}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
.

The association
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\cdot}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\cdot}}}
is a
binary relation over global types
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and typing contexts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
,
parameterised by multiparty sessions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.
There are two requirements for the association:

(1)

the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
must include an entry for each role
in the multiparty session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
; and

(2)

for each role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, the projection of the global type
onto this role (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}
) must be a subtype (
Definition

4
) of its corresponding entry in the
typing context (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}
).

Example 5
(Association)

Recall the global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
and its projected local types

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}
in
Example

3
.
Consider the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}
, where:

\small\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{login}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{fail}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{fatal}}}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{fatal}}}}\right\}}}}\end{array}

Intuitively,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
is associated with
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
, as
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
sends only

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}
, while
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
expect
to receive additional messages
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{fail}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{fatal}}
, respectively.
Indeed, the entries in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
adhere to the communication behaviour patterns of each role
of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
,
though with fewer output messages and more
input ones.

We can formally verify the association of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
by:

•

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}})}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}\right\}}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}\right\}}}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{login}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}})}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}\right)}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{login}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}\right)}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0mm}l@{\hskip 0mm}}.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\\
.0pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}\hfil\hskip 0.0pt\end{array}\right\}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}\right)}

We demonstrate the
operational correspondence
between a global type
and any
associated
typing context through
two main theorems:

Theorem

3.1
shows that the reducibility of a global type aligns with that of
its associated typing context; while

Theorem

3.2
illustrates that each possible reduction of a typing context is simulated by
an action in the reductions of the associated global type.

Theorem 3.1
(Soundness of Association)

Given associated global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}
,
then there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}}
, such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
.

Proof

By induction on global type reductions (
Definition

6
).

Theorem 3.2
(Completeness of Association)

Given
associated global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}
, then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.

Proof

By induction on typing context reductions (
Definition

8
).

Based on
Theorem

3.1
and
Theorem

3.2
,
we derive a corollary:
a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is in operational correspondence with the typing
context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}}\right\}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}}
, containing the projections of all roles in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
.

Example 6
(Soundness and Completeness of Association)

Consider the associated global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}

and typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
in
Example

5
.

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{login}}}}}
, and by
the soundness of association (
Theorem

3.1
),
there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{cancel}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}

such that

\small{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{cancel}}}}}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{quit}}}}}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}_{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}_{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}_{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}

where:

\small\begin{array}[]{lll}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}_{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}_{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}_{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{passwd}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{str}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{auth}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{bool}}})}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{fatal}}}}\right\}}}}\end{array}

By
Definition

9
, it is easy to check
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}}}
.
Further, we have the reduction for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}
:

\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\text{auth}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}

which, by the completeness of association (
Theorem

3.2
), relates to the reduction:

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{quit}}}}}\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\text{auth}}}}}
.

Remark 1
(‘Weakness’ and ‘Adequacy’ of Soundness Theorem)

Inquisitive readers might question why we opted to formulate a ‘
weak
’
soundness theorem
rather than one that mirrors the completeness theorem,
as seen in existing literature
[
6
]
.
This choice arises from our utilisation of the subtyping (
Definition

4
, notably

[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}
]
).
A local type in the typing context might offer fewer branches for selection
compared to the projected local type,
leading to transmission actions in the global type that remain uninhabited.

For example, consider the global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}

and its associated typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
, as shown in
Example

5
.
While the global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}
might transition through

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{login}}}
,
the associated typing context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
cannot.

Nonetheless,
our soundness theorem
adequately
ensures the desired properties
guaranteed via association, including safety, deadlock-freedom, and liveness,
as demonstrated in
Section

3.5
.

3.5
Typing Context Properties Guaranteed via Association

The design of multiparty session type theory
provides a substantial advantage in guaranteeing
desirable properties through its methodology.
Consequently, processes adhering to the local types obtained from projections are
inherently
correct by construction
.
This subsection highlights three key properties:

communication safety
,
deadlock-freedom
,
and
liveness
.
We demonstrate that these properties are ensured by typing contexts associated with global types.

Communication Safety.

We begin by introducing communication safety for typing contexts, a behavioural property that ensures each role can exchange compatible messages, thereby preventing label mismatches.

Definition 10
(Typing Context Safety)

Given a session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, we say that

\varphi
is an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety property
of typing contexts iff, whenever
\varphi\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)
, we have:

[S-
{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}}
]

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}}}}

and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}}})}}}}}

implies
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}}
;

[S-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
]

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}}

implies
\varphi\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}}}\right)
;

[S-
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
]

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

implies
\varphi\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)
.

We say
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe
, written
\operatorname{safe}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
, if
\varphi\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)
holds for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety property
\varphi
. We say
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is safe
, written
\operatorname{safe}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
, if
\varphi\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)
holds for some property
\varphi
which is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe
for all sessions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
occurring in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
.

Our safety property is derived from a fundamental feature of generalised MPST systems
[
19
, Def. 4.1]
.
As per
Definition

10
, safety is a
coinductive
property
[
18
]
.
This means that
for a given a session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe is the largest
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety property,
including the union of all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety properties.
To demonstrate the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety of a typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
,
we must identify a property
\varphi
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\in\varphi
,
and subsequently prove that
\varphi
qualifies as an
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety property.
More specifically, if such a
\varphi
exists, it can be formulated as a set containing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}

and all its reductums (via transition
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to^{\!*}}_{\!\!\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}
).
We then verify whether all elements of
\varphi
satisfy each clause of
Definition

10
.

According to
clause
[S-
{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}}
]
,
whenever two roles
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

attempt communication, the receiving role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is required to
accommodate all output messages of the
sending role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
with compatible payload types, ensuring the feasibility of the communication.
Clause
[S-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
]
unfolds any recursive entry, while
clause
[S-
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
]

specifies that any typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, to which
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
transitions on
session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, must also belong to
\varphi
, indicating that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe as well.
Note that any entry
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
vacuously
satisfies all clauses.

Example 7
(Typing Context Safety)

Consider the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
from
Example

5
.
We know that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe by verifying
its reductions. For example, we have the reductions

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{cancel}}}}}}\cdot\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{quit}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\text{auth}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
, where
each reductum complies with all clauses of
Definition

10
.

The typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{A}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}3}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}4}}}}
is
not

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe.
Any property
\varphi
containing such a typing
context is
not
a
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safety property, as it violates

[S-
{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}}
]
of
Definition

10
:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{A}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}}}}}
and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{A}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}}}}}
, but

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{A}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}}}

does not hold.

Finally, let’s consider the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{B}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{real}})}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}})}}}}
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{B}}
is
not

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe, as any property
\varphi
containing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{B}}

contradicts
[S-
{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}}
]
:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{B}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{real}}})}}}}}
and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{B}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})}}}}}
, but

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{B}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}}

is not uphold due to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{real}}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}
.

Deadlock-Freedom.

The property of deadlock-freedom determines
whether a typing context can keep reducing without getting stuck,
unless it reaches a successful terminal state.

Definition 11
(Deadlock-Free Typing Contexts)

Given a session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, a typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free
,
written
\operatorname{df}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
,
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to^{\!*}}_{\!\!\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\!\!\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}

implies
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
.

It is noteworthy
that a
typing context that reduces infinitely adheres to deadlock-freedom,
as it consistently undergoes further reductions.
Alternatively, when a terminal typing context is reached,
all entries in the typing context must be terminated successfully (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
).
Consequently, a deadlock-free typing context either continues perpetual reduction
or terminates.

Example 8
(Typing Context Deadlock-Freedom)

The typing
context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
in
Example

5
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free,
as any terminal typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}
, reached from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}

via

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to^{\!*}}_{\!\!\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{\text{auth}}}}\!\!\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
, only contains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
entries,
which can be easily verified.

The typing
context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{C}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}3}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}3}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}

is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe but
not

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free, as
its inputs and outputs, despite being dual, are arranged in the incorrect order.
Specifically, there are no possible reductions for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{C}}
, i.e.
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{C}}}\!\!\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
,
while none of the entries in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{C}}
is a termination.

Finally, the
context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{D}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}3}}\right\}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\right\}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}

is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free but
not

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe, as it consistently reaches a
successful termination by transmitting
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}
between
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
; while

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is unable to receive the message
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}3}}
when
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
intends to send it due to the message mismatch.

Liveness.

The liveness property ensures that every pending output/external
choice eventually gets triggered through a message transmission.
It relies on fairness, specifically based on
strong fairness of components

[
8
, Fact 2]
, to guarantee that every enabled message transmission is successfully executed. The definitions of fair and live paths for typing contexts are provided in
Definition

12
, and these paths are used to formalise the liveness for typing contexts in
Definition

13
.

Definition 12
(Fair, Live Paths)

A
path
is a
possibly infinite sequence of typing contexts
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}})_{n\in N}
, where
N=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
is a set of consecutive natural numbers, and,
\forall n\!\in\!N
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n+1}}
.
We say that a path
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}})_{n\in N}
is
fair for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
iff,
\forall n\!\in\!N
:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}}
implies
\exists k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}
such that
N\ni k\geq n
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{k+1}}
.

We say that a path
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}})_{n\in N}
is
live for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
iff,
\forall n\in N
:

(L1)

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}}}}
implies
\exists k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}
such that
N\ni k\geq n
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{k+1}}
;

(L2)

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{n}}}\!\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}})}}}}}
implies
\exists k,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}
such that
N\ni k\geq n
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{k}}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{k+1}}
.

A path is a (possibly infinite) sequence of reductions of a typing context.
A path is fair for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
if, along the path,
every enabled message transmission
is eventually performed on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.
Likewise, a path is live for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
if, along the path,
every pending internal/external choice is eventually triggered on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.

Definition 13
(Live Typing Contexts)

Given a session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, a typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live
,
written
\operatorname{live}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
, iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to^{\!*}}_{\!\!\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
implies all paths starting with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

that are fair for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
are also live for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.

A typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live if any reductum of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
(via
transition
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to^{\!*}}_{\!\!\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}
) consistently generates a live path for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
under fairness.

Example 9
(Fairness and Typing Context Liveness, originated from
[
7
,
19
]
)

Consider the typing context:
