\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}}}

which is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free.
There is an infinite path starting with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{E}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}
keep communicating in session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
while
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}
never trigger a reduction to interact, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{E}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{E}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}}}\,\cdots
.
Such a path is
not
fair for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
because
although message transmission between
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}
is enabled, it will never occur.
Alternatively, along any fair path of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{E}}
,
all inputs and outputs
can eventually be fired on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, indicating that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{E}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live.

Now consider another typing context:

\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}.{{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\end{array}\right\}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}.{{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}\end{array}\right\}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}}}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}

which is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{E}}
has fair and live paths, where in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}
is transmitted from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, and then to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
However,
there is also a fair path,
where in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}
is consistently transmitted from

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{E}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{E}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}}}\,\cdots
.
In this case,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
indefinitely awaits input that will never arrive.
Therefore, while this path is fair for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, it is not live, and hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{E}}
is not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live.

Finally, the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\right\}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}3}}\right\}}}}

is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live: there is only one path starting from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{E}}
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{E}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
, which is fair and live for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
; while it is not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe.

Properties by Association.

We conclude by demonstrating, as stated in
Theorem

3.3
,
that a typing context associated with a global type is
constructed to possess the properties of safety, deadlock-freedom, and liveness.

Theorem 3.3
(Safety, Deadlock-Freedom, and Liveness by Association)

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
be a global type,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
a typing context, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
a session.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is associated with
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live.

Example 10
(Typing Context Properties Guaranteed by Association)

The typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
described in
Example

5

is associated with
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}

for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{auth}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}}}
.
Consequently,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
possesses the desirable properties of
being
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free (as also demonstrated in
Example

7
and
Example

8
, respectively),
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live.

3.6
Relationships Between Typing Context Properties

We now explore both the relationships between
typing context properties, and how they relate to association,
as formalised in
Theorem

3.4
below.

Theorem 3.4

For any typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
and session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, the following
statements
are valid:

(1)

\operatorname{df}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})\mathrel{{\mathrel{{\kern 10.00002pt}{\not{\kern-10.00002pt}\impliedby}}}\,{\mathrel{\not\!\!\!\implies}}}\operatorname{safe}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
;

(2)

\operatorname{live}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})\mathrel{{\mathrel{{\kern 10.00002pt}{\not{\kern-10.00002pt}\impliedby}}}\,{\mathrel{\not\!\!\!\implies}}}\operatorname{safe}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
;

(3)

\operatorname{live}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})\mathrel{{\mathrel{{\kern 10.00002pt}{\not{\kern-10.00002pt}\impliedby}}}\!\!{\implies}}\operatorname{df}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
;

(4)

\exists{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}

\mathrel{{\mathrel{{\kern 10.00002pt}{\not{\kern-10.00002pt}\impliedby}}}\!\!{\implies}}

\operatorname{safe}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
;

(5)

\exists{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}

\mathrel{{\mathrel{{\kern 10.00002pt}{\not{\kern-10.00002pt}\impliedby}}}\!\!{\implies}}

\operatorname{df}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
;

(6)

\exists{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}

\mathrel{{\mathrel{{\kern 10.00002pt}{\not{\kern-10.00002pt}\impliedby}}}\!\!{\implies}}

\operatorname{live}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})
.

In the diagram, the “safe” set (resp. “df” set, “live” set )
contains all typing contexts that
are
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe (resp.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live).
The red set
\mathbb{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}
encompasses
all typing contexts associated with some global type for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.
The negated implications stated in
Theorem

3.4

are showcased in
Example

8
,
Example

9
, and

Example

11
(below), respectively.

Example 11
(Non-Associated Typing Context, originated from
[
2
,
19
]
)

The typing context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{F}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}})}}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
(from
[
2
, Ex. 1.2]
)
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-live (and hence
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-deadlock-free and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
-safe), but
not
associated with any global type for session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
:
this is because a recursion variable
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}
occurs as payload in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, which is not allowed by either
Definition

3
or
Definition

4
.

4
Multiparty Session Typing System

This section presents a type system for the multiparty session
\pi
-calculus,
as explained in
Section

2
.
We introduce the typing rules in
Section

4.1
,
and show the main properties of typed processes: subject reduction
and session fidelity, in
Section

4.2
.
Finally, we demonstrate how process properties such as
deadlock-freedom
and liveness
can be guaranteed by construction in
Section

4.3
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{n}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\right)={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{n}}}\qquad\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{1}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\ldots\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{n}}}}})\lx@proof@logical@and\forall i\in 1\ldots n\text{${\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}$ is basic \;or\; }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\\[2.84526pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}})\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathcal{B}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{1}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{1}}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{2}}}}\\[2.84526pt]

Figure 7
:

Typing rules for processes.

4.1
Typing Rules

Two kinds of typing contexts, as introduced in
Definition

7
, are used in our type system:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}
, responsible for assigning an
n
-tuple of types to each process variable
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}
(one type per argument),
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
, which maps variables to payload types (basic types or session types),
as well as channels with roles (endpoints) to session types.
These typing contexts are jointly applied in judgments formulated as:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}

(with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}
omitted when empty)

which interprets as:
“considering the process types in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
uses its variables and channels
linearly

based on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
.”
This
typing judgement
is defined
by the rules in
Figure

7
, where, for convenience,
we include type annotations for channels bound by process definitions and restrictions.

The main innovation in
Figure

7
lies in rule
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\nu}}
]
,
which uses a typing context associated with a global type in some session

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
(
Definition

9
) to enforce session restrictions.
The rest of rules are mostly standard
[
19
]
.
Rule
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}
]
retrieves
process variables, while
rule
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}
]
types a value
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}

if it belongs to a set of basic types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathcal{B}}
.
Rule
[T-Sub]
applies subtyping within a singleton typing context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}}}
when assigning type

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}}
to a variable or channel
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
.
Additionally, rule
[T-
\operatorname{end}
]

introduces a predicate
\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\cdot})
for typing
contexts, denoting the termination of all endpoints.
This predicate is used in

[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
]
to type an inactive process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
.
Rules
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\oplus}
]
and
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\&}
]
assign
selection and branching types to channels used by selection and branching processes,
respectively. Rules
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}}
]
and
[T-Call]
deal with
recursive processes declarations and calls, respectively.
Notably, the clauses “
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\not\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
”
used in rules
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\oplus}
]
and
[T-Call]

prevent the sending or passing of channels typed as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
,
while permitting the sending/passing of channels and data of any other type.
Finally, rule
[T-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}
]

linearly
divides the typing context into two parts,
each used to type one sub-process.

Example 12
(Typed Process)

Consider the processes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
from
Example

1
.
The type context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{H}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}
, with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
, can type the process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
by the following
derivation:

\scriptsize\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{H}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\lx@proof@logical@and\Omega{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}{}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb] for session

4.2
Subject Reduction and Session Fidelity

We elucidate the key properties of well-typed processes, including

subject reduction
(
Theorem

4.1
),

session fidelity
(
Theorem

4.2
).

Subject reduction

ensures the preservation of types during process reduction.
It states that if
a well-typed process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
reduces to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
, then the reduction is simulated
by the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
used to type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
.
Note that in our subject reduction theorem,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
is
not
required to contain only one single session.
Instead,
we include all restricted sessions in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
, ensuring that reductions on
these various sessions maintain their respective restrictions. This is enforced by rule
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\nu}}
]

in
Figure

7
. Additionally, the theorem dictates that,
in alignment with the MPST top-down methodology,
a process initially constructed from global types retains this construction
manner even after reductions.

Theorem 4.1
(Subject Reduction)

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
where
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}:\exists{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
, then

\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{}^{\!\!\!*}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}}}
, and
\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}:\exists{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}}
.

Proof

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
(
Definition

2
).

As a corollary from
Theorem

4.1
, we have that well-typed processes communicate without
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}
ors.

Corollary 1
(Type Safety)

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\emptyset}\cdot{\emptyset}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\!\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
has no error.

Example 13
(Subject Reduction)

Take the typed process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
and
the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{H}}
from
Example

12
.
After a reduction using
[R-
\oplus\&
]
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
transitions to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}({x})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}{}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{42}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}}
.
The typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{H}}

can reduce to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{H}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
, via
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\&}
]
,
which can be used to type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}}
, and is associated with a global
type

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{H}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\operatorname{int}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}}}
.
In fact, by applying
Theorem

4.1
, we can infer that all process
reductions initiated from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
maintain well-typedness. Furthermore,
based on
Corollary

1
,
we are ensured that they are error-free.

Observe that the typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\text{auth}}}
from
Examples

5
and
12

and the process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}

from
Examples

2
and
12
also adhere to subject reduction, ensuring type safety.

Session fidelity
asserts the converse implication concerning subject reduction:
if a process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
is typed by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
can reduce along session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
can replicate at least one of the reductions performed by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
(although not necessarily all such reductions,
as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
over-approximates the behaviour of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
).
Consequently, we can deduce
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
’s behaviour from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
’s behaviour,
as demonstrated in
Theorem

4.3
.
However, this outcome does
not
hold universally for all well-typed processes:
a well-typed process might loop in a recursion, such as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}(\ldots)={X}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}}
, or it could deadlock by intricately interleaving communications across multiple sessions
[
5
]
. To address this, similarly to
[
19
]
and most session type works, we establish session fidelity specifically for processes featuring guarded recursion
and implementing a single multiparty session as a parallel composition of one sub-process per role.
The formalisation of session fidelity is provided in
Theorem

4.2
below,
building upon the concepts introduced in
Definition

14
.

Definition 14
(from
[
19
]
)

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
. We say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
:

(1)

has guarded definitions

iff
in each process definition in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
of the form

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{1}}}},...,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{n}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{n}}}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}}}
,
for all
i\in 1..n
,
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}
is a session type, then a call

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Y}}\!\left\langle{...,x_{i},...}\right\rangle}

can only occur in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}

as a subterm of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{x_{i}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{j}}}}}({y_{j}})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{j}}}}\}_{j\in J}}

or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{x_{i}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime\prime}}}}}{}

(i.e. after using
x_{i}
for input or output);

(2)

only plays role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
iff:
​(i)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
has guarded definitions;  ​(ii)

\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\right)\!=\!\emptyset
;
​(iii)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!=\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{0}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}

with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\not\leqslant}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}

and

\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{0}}})
;
​(iv)
for all subterms

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s^{\prime}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
,

\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}})
.

We say “
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
only plays role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
”
iff

\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
,
and item
(2)
holds.

In
Definition

14
, item
(1)

formalises guarded recursion for processes, while
item
(2)
identifies a process that plays
exactly
one
role on
one
session.
It is evident that
an ensemble of such processes
cannot deadlock
by waiting for each other on multiple sessions.

Example 14
(Playing Only Role)

Consider the processes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
from
Example

1
,
and the typing context

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{H}}
from
Example

12
.
Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
does not only play either
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.
This arises from the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
can only be typed
by a context of the form

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}
, where
neither
\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}})
nor

\operatorname{end}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})
holds,
thus violating item
(2)
of
Definition

14
.
Conversely,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
only plays role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
,
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{H_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}}
,
as all conditions are fulfilled.

Note that the process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}
(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}
) from
Examples

2
and
12

only plays role
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
(resp.
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}
)
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, which can be easily verified.

We now formalise our session fidelity result (
Theorem

4.2
).
Although the statement appears similar to Thm. 5.4 in
[
19
]
,
it utilises a typing context associated with a global type for a specific session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
to type the process.
This approach guarantees not only the fulfilment of the single-session requirements for
processes (
Definition

14
), but also asserts that a process constructed from a
global type maintains this structure even after reductions.

Theorem 4.2
(Session Fidelity)

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\!}\cdot{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\equiv\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in I}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}=\bigcup_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
such that for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
: (1)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\!}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}
,
and
(2) either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
, or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
only plays
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
. Then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
implies

\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\!}\cdot{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}}}
, with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\equiv\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in I}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}=\bigcup_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
such that for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
:
(1)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\!}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}
, and
(2) either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
,
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
only plays
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
.

Proof

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}
(
Definition

8
).

Example 15
(Guarded Definitions in Session Fidelity, from
[
19
]
)

According to rule
[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}}
]
in
Figure

7
, an unguarded definition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\!\left\langle{x}\right\rangle}}}
can be typed with
any

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}
. Therefore, for example, we have:

\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{def}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\mathbin{\!:\!}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\!\left\langle{x}\right\rangle}}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{X}\!\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right\rangle}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}}}}}}}

The above unguarded process reduces trivially
by invoking
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}
infinitely, without triggering any typing context reduction. This clarifies the necessity of guarded definitions in
Theorem

4.2
.

4.3
Properties of Typed Processes

We showcase that processes constructed from global types guarantee desirable run-time properties,
including deadlock-freedom and liveness, as formalised in
Definition

15
.
These properties are relatively
straightforward:

deadlock-freedom
indicates that if a process is unable to reduce,
then it consists only of inactive sub-processes (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
);

liveness
ensures that if a process attempts to perform an input or
output, it will eventually succeed.

Definition 15
(Runtime Process Properties)

We say
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
is:

(1)

deadlock-free
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\!\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}}\!\not{\!\!\!\to}

implies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
;

(2)

live
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\!\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\!\equiv\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}}}\!\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\right]

implies:

(a)

if

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\oplus\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{m}}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}}\rangle\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}}}}}

then
\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}^{\prime}}:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}^{\prime}}}\!\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}}}\right]
;

(b)

if

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]\mathbin{\!\&\!}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}}}({x_{i}})\vphantom{x}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}

then

\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}^{\prime}},k\in I,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P^{\prime}}\mathrel{\to{}^{\!\!\!*}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{C}^{\prime}}}\!\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q^{\prime}_{k}}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}}}{{x_{k}}}}\right\}}}\right]
.

We conclude by illustrating
how a process, typed with a typing context
associated to a global type on a session,
ensures both deadlock-freedom and liveness.

Theorem 4.3
(Process Deadlock-Freedom, Liveness)

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\!}\cdot{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\equiv\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in I}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}
,  and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}=\bigcup_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
such that for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\emptyset}\!}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}
.
Further, assume that each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}

is either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
(up to
\equiv
), or only plays
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
, by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
is deadlock-free and live.

Example 16
(Typed Process Properties)

The process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}}

from
Examples

2
and
12
is deadlock-free and live,
verified by
applying either
Definition

15
or
Theorem

4.3
.

5
Conclusion

This paper corrects a misunderstanding in multiparty session type theory,
proposing a new relation,
association

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}

between a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and a set of
local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
.
The top-down typing system, with
the association between global types
and their end-point projections given by mergeability,
can guarantee
type safety
,
deadlock-freedom
, and
liveness
of session processes
by construction
.

Appendix 0.A
Proofs for
Section

3

With regard to recursive global types, we define their
unfolding
as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}\right)}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
otherwise.
A recursive type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
must be guarded (or
contractive), i.e. the unfolding leads to a progressive prefix, e.g. a communication.
Unguarded types, such as
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}^{\prime}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}}
, are excluded.
Similar definitions and requirements apply for local types.

0.A.1
Unfoldings

Lemma 1

For a closed, well-guarded global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}
can only be of
form
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
, or
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{\cdots}}}}\right\}}}
.

For a closed, well-guarded local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}
can only be
of form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{\cdots\right\}}
, or

{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\cdots\right\}}
.

Proof

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}
will not appear since we require closed types.

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime}}}\mathord{\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime}}}
since we require well-guarded types (recursive
types are contractive).
Similar argument for local types.

Lemma 2

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}
.

Proof

By the definition of projection (
Definition

3
),
the definitions of unfolding recursive global and local types,
and
Lemma

1
.

0.A.2
Subtyping

Lemma 3
(Subtyping is Reflexive)

For any closed, well-guarded local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
holds.

Proof

We construct a relation
R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})\right\}}}
.
It is trivial to show that
R
satisfies all clauses
of
Definition

4
, and hence,
R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.

Lemma 4
(Subtyping is Transitive)

For any closed, well-guarded local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}
,
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}
holds.

Proof

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}})}\,\middle|\,{\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\text{ such that }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}}\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.

Lemma 5

For any local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
if and
only if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
.

Proof

By
Definition

4
.

Lemma 6

For any closed, well-guarded local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,

1.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
; and

2.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}
.

Proof

1.

By
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
R]
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}
. Otherwise,
by reflexivity (
Lemma

3
).

2.

By
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
L]
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}
. Otherwise,
by reflexivity (
Lemma

3
).

Lemma 7

Given a collection of mergable local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
For all
j\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}
holds.

Proof

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}})}\,\middle|\,{j\in I}\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.

Lemma 8

Given a collection of mergable local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
If for all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}
for some local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}
.

Proof

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U})\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.

Lemma 9

Given a collection of mergable local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
If for all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
for some local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}
.

Proof

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}})\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.

Lemma 10

Given two collections of mergable local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
If for all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}
.

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}}})\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.

Lemma 11
(Inversion of Subtyping)

1.

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
, and
I\subseteq J
,
and
\forall i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
.

2.

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
, and
J\subseteq I
,
and
\forall i\in J:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
.

Proof

By
Lemmas

1
and
6
, the transitivity of subtyping, and
Definition

4
(
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}
]
,
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}
]
).

0.A.3
Semantics of Global Types

Lemma 12

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
iff
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.

Proof

By inverting or applying
[GR-
\mu
]
when necessary.

Lemma 13
(Progress of Global Types)

If
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
(where
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is a projectable
global type),
then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.

Proof

By
Lemma

12
, we only consider unfoldings.

•

Case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
: the premise does not hold;

•

Case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{S}_{i}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
: apply
[GR-
\oplus\&
]
to reduce the global type.

0.A.4
Semantics of Typing Contexts

Lemma 14

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)}
.

Proof

By induction on typing context reductions.

Lemma 15

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\right\}}}
, then
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
with

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}
.

Proof

By induction on typing context reductions.

Lemma 16
(Inversion of Typing Context Reduction)

1.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
,
k\in I
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}
;

2.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\right)}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
,
k\in I
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}
.

Proof

By applying and inverting
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}
]
,
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}
]
, and
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
\mu
]
(when necessary).

Lemma 17
(Determinism of Typing Context Reduction)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}
.

Proof

By induction on typing context reductions.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}

and

Lemma 20

1.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then for any channel with role
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
.

2.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}:{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}})}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then for any channel with role
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
.

3.

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}]}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
then for any channel with role
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}]}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}]}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
.
